Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 30 October 2026, 08:51 to 09:11 UK, Fable, richie login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Sets 3 and 4 answers: the predictability of the forecasts, and what guidance, the sell-side and the narrative already reflect

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Schneider Electric SE (SU-FR), written 2026-10-29, price EUR 231.40 (23-Oct-26)

Preliminary notes for the Consumer: what the new evidence changes, and the cross-check gaps in the model summary

These notes are not answers. They record the facts that arrived with the AlphaSense reports written for this work, where they alter the second set's footing, and how I read the gaps between the second set's own cross-check and the Financial Analyst's model. The answers below use the corrected footing.

1. The equity raise is now a fact, and it is larger than the second set modelled. The company's prospectus supplement of 21 October 2026 records 25,000,000 new shares placed at EUR 240, gross proceeds of EUR 6.0bn, a 4.99% discount to the EUR 252.60 close of 19 October, equal to 4.32% of the pre-issue capital. The second set modelled 22.9m shares and EUR 5.5bn. Issued shares were 577.1m at 30 June 2026; after the July 2026 employee plan (1.5m shares) and the placement they are 603.6m, and net of 14.8m treasury shares about 588.8m are outstanding. I now carry about 587.5m shares at end-2026 (after the remaining EUR 600m buyback, to be completed in November 2026, and performance shares), an average of about 589m in FY2027 and 591m in FY2028, against the second set's 586.5m and 588.9m. That alone takes about 0.4% off FY2027 and FY2028 EPS. The net proceeds (about EUR 5.9bn after fees) are about EUR 0.45bn higher than the second set's EUR 5.44bn, which slightly lowers the debt need.

2. The cost of the acquisition debt is higher than the second set assumed, and the first EUR 6bn is already on the balance sheet. On 28 October 2026 Schneider priced a EUR 6.0bn multi-tranche bond at an average coupon of about 4.3%: a EUR 750m floating tranche to 2028 at three-month Euribor plus 0.45%, and fixed tranches of 4.00% (2030), 4.25% (2032), 4.50% (2035), 4.75% (2038) and 5.00% (2046), average maturity 8.2 years. The second set used 3.75% on EUR 16.5bn; Deutsche Bank and Redburn use 3.5%; Bernstein's model carries net interest of only EUR 466m in 2027 and EUR 343m in 2028. The remaining roughly EUR 10bn has not been launched and is to be issued "across several currencies"; A-rated dollar issuers paid 5.65% to 5.90% in October 2026 (CBRE), so a part-dollar mix pushes the blended cost of the full package to about 4.5% to 4.8%. I now use 4.6% on about EUR 16bn, and I carry the negative carry on the EUR 6bn already issued from November 2026 until closing, largely offset by interest earned on the roughly EUR 12bn of pre-funding cash. Net financial items become about −EUR 1,040m (2027), −1,480m (2028), −1,350m (2029) and −1,230m (2030), against the second set's −1,020m, −1,360m, −1,240m and −1,130m. Each further 100 basis points on the new debt is about EUR 160m before tax, or about 1.5% of EPS.

3. The PTC closing date stays at 1 October 2027 in my base. The company says "by the third quarter of 2027"; Deutsche Bank consolidates from Q4 2027; Redburn from 1 July 2027; Bernstein takes one quarter. A slip into 2028 (the PTC shareholder vote, HSR and CFIUS are outstanding) would lower FY2027 EPS by a further 1% to 1.5% (shares without the profit), while lowering peak leverage.

4. My revised base case for adjusted EPS (brokers' basis) is EUR 10.36 (2026), 11.77 (2027), 13.54 (2028), 15.19 (2029) and 16.94 (2030), against the second set's 10.36, 11.85, 13.73, 15.37 and 17.11 and consensus of 10.30, 12.61, 14.49 and 16.11. Operating lines are unchanged: organic growth 13.9%, 10.5%, 8.0%, 7.5%, 7.5%; adjusted EBITA margin 19.9%, 20.5%, 21.7%, 22.3%, 22.7%. The whole of the revision is below the operating line (shares and interest), which is exactly where the second set said the risk sat. My FY2027 EPS is now about 6.7% below consensus, FY2028 about 6.6% below, FY2029 about 5.7% below.

5. The support-function story is confirmed in the company's own numbers, and it is harder than the second set stated. Absolute support-function costs were EUR 7,859m (FY2022), 8,600m (FY2023, +9.4%), 9,185m (FY2024) and 9,375m (FY2025, +2.1%); in H1 2026 they rose 4.9% to EUR 4,921m while the ratio fell from 24.3% to 23.2% because revenue grew faster. The FY2025 bridge shows gross savings of EUR 349m against EUR 310m of wage and research inflation and EUR 438m of strategic investment. Support costs rose as a share of sales in 2023 and 2024 despite explicit targets (Morgan Stanley). The second set's "freeze, not cut" reading is right, and absolute costs are still growing; the ratio falls only while revenue grows faster than about 5% a year.

6. The equity cross-check gaps in the model summary, explained. The summary's Part 4 table compares the second set's own EPS and free cash flow with the model's. The gaps and my reading:

  • EPS, FY2026: 10.36 against 10.25 (−1.1%). Net income is almost identical (EUR 5,871m against 5,879m), so the gap is the share count: the model's EPS implies about 573.6m average shares, the Analyst's 566.7m. The model applies a mid-year convention to the "buybacks less new shares issued for cash" line, so it weights the late-October placement at about half a year. This is a convention, not a wrong input; the true FY2026 figure is close to the Analyst's 10.36. With my corrected proceeds (note 7), the model's FY2026 EPS will read about 10.2; the Consumer should treat FY2026 EPS as about EUR 10.3 to 10.4.
  • EPS, FY2027 to FY2030: within 0.2% to 0.7%. Not material; the FY2030 gap (17.11 against 17.23) is the model retiring slightly more shares with the 2030 buyback. No input change follows from these years; my own revision (notes 1, 2 and 7) is a change of view, not a reconciliation.
  • Free cash flow, FY2026 to FY2030: the model is lower by EUR 162m (3.6%), 218m (4.0%), 442m (6.5%), 91m (1.2%) and 123m (1.4%). Two sources. First, a recurring gap of roughly EUR 100m to 160m a year: the Analyst's cross-check is built from operating cash flow, which adds back non-cash charges (share-based payment, which I estimate at about EUR 150m a year, and provision movements), while the model's equity free cash flow starts from net income and D&A and does not. This is a definitional convention and I leave it. Second, and this is the input that is wrong, the model charges working capital at 14.8% of total revenue, so the acquired revenue it consolidates (about EUR 1,060m of run-rate scope in FY2027, about EUR 2,850m cumulatively in FY2028) creates a working-capital outflow of about EUR 140m in FY2027 and EUR 250m in FY2028 that does not exist: acquired working capital is paid for in the purchase price, and PTC bills in advance. The Analyst flagged this caution but left the input unchanged. I correct it by lowering the working-capital ratio to the level that holds the organic business at 14.8% of its own revenue: 14.8% (2026), 14.5% (2027), 14.1% (2028), 14.0% (2029), 14.0% (2030) (see inputs-changed.md). After this the FY2027 and FY2028 free-cash-flow gaps should fall to about EUR 80m and EUR 190m, which is the definitional residue.

7. The three inputs I change (full lines in inputs-changed.md): net financial items (note 2); buybacks less new shares issued for cash in FY2026, from +5,110 to +5,570 (25m shares at EUR 240, about EUR 5.9bn net, less the EUR 600m buyback, plus EUR 270m from the employee plan); and the working-capital ratio (note 6). I do not change revenue growth, EBITDA margins, D&A, tax, dividends, capital spending, leases or the acquisitions line. On tax I note that Redburn expects the group rate to stay near 23% because PTC's own effective rate is 21%, against the second set's 24.8% from 2028; each point is about 1.3% of EPS, so the second set's choice is conservative by up to about 2% of EPS from 2028. I leave it, because interest deductibility and the global minimum tax push in opposite directions and the company's own range is 23% to 25%.

8. A reading note on the three valuation readings in the files. The FactSet 24-month-forward P/E is 16.33x (11th percentile of five years); the model's two-year-forward P/E is 18.0x; the dashboard's current P/E is 21.3x (77th percentile of ten years). On my EPS (about EUR 13.1 for the 24-month-forward blend) the forward multiple at EUR 231.40 is about 17.7x, the 25th percentile of five years, not the 11th. Part of the stock's apparent cheapness rests on consensus EPS that has not absorbed the share count and the interest cost.

The third set: assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: Risks · Group: Guidance · Group: Sell-side estimates · Group: Stock market narrative

The Investment Analyst's heading (Layer 4) The fourth set: the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative
Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Forecast inputs changed by the third and fourth sets: Schneider Electric SE (SU-FR), as at 29-Oct-26

Money in millions of EUR, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the lines changed are shown; every other line in files/04-forecast-inputs-from-the-second-set.md stands.

Part 1. Profit and loss inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Net financial items -820 -1040 -1480 -1350 -1230

The EUR 6.0bn bond priced on 28 October 2026 carries an average coupon of about 4.3% against the 3.75% the second set assumed, and the remaining roughly EUR 10bn is to be issued partly in dollars where A-rated issuers paid 5.65% to 5.90% in October 2026, so I now carry about 4.6% on about EUR 16bn of new debt plus the carry on the EUR 6bn already issued, which raises the line by about EUR 20m in 2027 and EUR 100m to 120m a year from 2028.

Part 2. Cash flow and balance sheet inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Working capital (% of revenue) 14.8 14.5 14.1 14.0 14.0

The model applies this ratio to total revenue including the acquired revenue of Cognite, AiDASH, Shelly and PTC (about EUR 1,060m of run-rate scope in FY2027 and about EUR 2,850m cumulatively from FY2028), which charges a working-capital outflow of about EUR 140m in FY2027 and EUR 250m in FY2028 that does not exist because acquired working capital is paid for in the purchase price and PTC bills in advance; the lowered ratios hold the organic business at 14.8% of its own revenue (14.7% and 14.6% in 2029 and 2030 as before) and remove the model's spurious outflow, which explains most of the free-cash-flow gap between the second set's cross-check and the model in FY2027 and FY2028.

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Buybacks less new shares issued for cash 5570 270 270 -830 -1030

The prospectus supplement of 21 October 2026 records 25,000,000 new shares placed at EUR 240 for gross proceeds of EUR 6.0bn (about EUR 5.9bn net of fees), against the 22.9m shares and EUR 5.44bn net the second set assumed, so FY2026 becomes -600 (buyback) + 5,900 (net placement proceeds) + 270 (employee share plan) = +5,570; the later years are unchanged, and the Consumer should note that the model's mid-year convention on this line understates FY2026 EPS by about 1% to 1.5% (the true FY2026 figure is about EUR 10.3 to 10.4) while correctly carrying the full-year count of about 589m average shares into FY2027.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of SU-FR with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-28 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Peers (from the dashboard data file): ABB (ABBN-CH), Alfen (ALFEN-NL), Arteche (ART-ES), Cenergy (CENER-GR), Cembre (CMB-IT), Siemens Energy (ENR-DE), Fastned (FAST-NL), Kempower (KEMPOWR-FI), Landis+Gyr (LAND-CH), Legrand (LR-FR), Nexans (NEX-FR), NKT (NKT-DK), Pfisterer (PFSE-DE), Prysmian (PRY-IT), R&S Group Holding (RSGN-CH), Siemens (SIE-DE)

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Schneider Electric SE (SU-FR): global energy-management and industrial-automation group; segments are Energy Management (low- and medium-voltage electrical distribution, secure power and data-centre systems, liquid cooling, building and grid software; about 83% of revenue) and Industrial Automation (discrete, process and hybrid automation hardware plus industrial software including AVEVA, with the Cognite and PTC acquisitions pending).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Schneider Electric (SU-FR), what are investors, as distinct from the sell-side, saying about the company since the PTC acquisition was announced on 5 October 2026 and the EUR 6.0bn accelerated bookbuild of 25 million shares at EUR 240 was completed on 21 October 2026 (company prospectus supplement, 21 October 2026)? Cover buy-side commentary in fund letters, investor conference feedback, broker-hosted investor surveys and polls, activist or shareholder statements, and social media (X/Twitter, Reddit, Substack, Seeking Alpha, LinkedIn): which arguments dominate (the PTC price and synergies, the dilution and leverage, the data-centre cycle and its financing, the pricing cycle, the 800-volt architecture risk, the margin programme), how positioning is described (overweight, trimming, exiting, waiting), and which of these dynamics investors say they are tracking into the FY2026 results on 18 February 2027. Separately, for every house that publishes estimates on Schneider in FactSet (including UBS, Goldman Sachs, Barclays, Berenberg, RBC, Santander, AlphaValue, Banco Sabadell, CIC/ESN, Oxcap, Panmure Liberum, Morningstar and Morgan Stanley, as well as Deutsche Bank, Bernstein, Jefferies and Rothschild & Co Redburn), state the date of its latest model, the share count it uses for 2027, the date from which it consolidates PTC, and the interest cost it assumes on the acquisition debt, so that it is clear which estimates reflect the 25 million new shares and the EUR 6.0bn bond priced on 28 October 2026 at an average coupon of about 4.3% (company press release, 28 October 2026).

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Schneider Electric (SU-FR), what have S&P Global Ratings (A/A-1, stable, reaffirmed 6 October 2026) and Moody's (A2, stable, reaffirmed 6 October 2026) stated as the leverage, funds-from-operations and deleveraging thresholds consistent with the current ratings after the PTC acquisition, and what have they said would trigger a downgrade or a negative outlook? What currency mix, tenors and instruments has the company announced or indicated for the remaining acquisition debt beyond the EUR 6.0bn multi-tranche bond of 28 October 2026 (including any US-dollar bonds, commercial paper or term loans, and the USD 25bn bridge facility underwritten by Morgan Stanley and Societe Generale, PTC 8-K of 5 October 2026), and what have comparable A-rated European industrial issuers paid for US-dollar bonds of five to twenty years since September 2026? What is the status and expected timetable of the PTC shareholder vote, HSR clearance and CFIUS review, and what do the precedents of large European acquisitions of US software companies show about the time from signing to closing and the frequency of delays beyond the guided closing quarter?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Schneider Electric (SU-FR), how has the share price and sell-side consensus reacted on and in the month after each February full-year results day from 2022 to 2026 when the initial organic growth and margin guidance for the new year was set below the sell-side's expectation (for example, the 7% to 10% organic growth guidance for 2026 given on 26 February 2026, later raised to 10% to 13% on 30 July 2026 and 12% to 14% on 16 October 2026), and how did the shares and consensus then move at the half-year results when guidance was raised? Across European electrical and capital-goods peers (ABB, Siemens, Legrand, Siemens Energy, Eaton, Vertiv) over 2021 to 2026, what has happened to the share price and to the forward multiple in the six months after a company (a) raised its mid-term capital-markets-day targets within eighteen months of setting them, as Legrand did on 29 September 2026 (2027 to 2030 organic growth 6% to 8%, adjusted operating margin 21% to 22%), and (b) reported results in which revenue and operating profit beat consensus while adjusted earnings per share missed because of a higher share count, higher interest or acquisition timing, and what distinguished the cases where the multiple recovered from those where it did not?
